# Samba second-campaign inventory and adaptations

Subsequent [multichannel/durable implementation](multichannel-durable-reconnect.md)
extends these historical adaptations. Current SMB311 behavior, terminal-binding
GSS retirement, valid async fixture corrections and remaining source differences
are documented there; original source mappings and results below are retained.

Pinned source: [`2816d12d5346c4e6258d8263f68eb653fd44fd5e`](https://github.com/samba-team/samba/tree/2816d12d5346c4e6258d8263f68eb653fd44fd5e/source4/torture/smb2).
The survey contains every file named by that directory's `wscript_build`: 48 C
files, plus the build manifest and three headers (52 files). All 48 downloaded C
files were checked against their Git blob IDs in the pinned recursive tree, as
well as SHA-256. Prior-campaign evidence was read only; new copies, supplementary
headers, builds, captures and external-client logs belong to campaign
`upstream-broad-20261108T044351Z`, under its `samba/` directory. Ordinary builds
have no dependency on this evidence or Samba.

`UpstreamSambaTests.Cases()` registers **751 cases** under `upstream.samba.` in two
partial-class files. The latest Linux .NET 10 Release JIT run passed **586/751**;
**165 failed**. No production or test-source drift occurred during that capture.
No production changes were made by this implementation task. No native Windows
or Native AOT result is claimed here; the parent owns those integration gates.
All new cases reach the public signed SMB dispatcher or real TCP boundary;
backend bytes are only corroboration. Advanced grants are obtained with CREATE
contexts, not by seeding a private caching/recovery manager. Direct-session
fixtures establish authentication/session state before dispatch and do not prove
external-client interoperability.

The scope update includes observable authentication, SPNEGO/NTLM, session/key
lifetime, signing and wire validation. These are retained in the inventory even
where they are still unported. There is no blanket auth/security exclusion.
Kerberos ticket-expiry cases require concrete KDC/domain infrastructure that is
not present; that limitation does not apply to the isolated NTLM cases.

## Selection and independent expected results

| Group | New registered cases | Observable contract and exact prior overlap |
|---|---:|---|
| Share/access | 324 | All 163 unique tuples from 169 source table positions, in both acquisition orders, plus stat-open/share-none. Three exact existing rows are omitted: forward R/WRITE is `upstream.create.share.existing-denies-write-retry`; reverse W/READ is `upstream.create.share.new-denies-existing-read-retry`; forward RW/DELETE is `upstream.create.share.delete-conflict-close-recreate`. EA/attribute/control/synchronize access bits are sharing inputs, not private-manager tests. |
| Attributes | 120 | Fifteen CREATE masks; seven initial by fifteen requested overwrite masks, fresh objects with known nonempty data. Successful overwrite checks response/query attributes and EOF; rejection checks original bytes. The source's stateful 16-by-16 sequence is inventoried separately and is not claimed identical to these independent partitions. Source Windows/DOS policy and Linux attribute support require serial review; the tests preserve the original expected values. |
| Directory CREATE | 13 | Five missing/existing forbidden-disposition rows (existing OVERWRITE already `upstream.create.semantic.directory-overwrite`), four invalid option combinations, collision precedence against a file, absent parent, 255-character component and temporary attribute rejection. |
| Other CREATE | 34 | Reserved request fields ignored; truncate while a byte range remains locked; 30 nondestructive option bits; two reserved DesiredAccess values must fail without creating an object. DELETE_ON_CLOSE is separately covered; obsolete undefined option 0x80 is classified as a source/spec mismatch, not asserted as NOT_SUPPORTED. |
| Streams | 28 | Six dispositions against existing/missing named streams; three base truncation/supersede rows; four legal name forms; three invalid type forms; implicit base creation; independent sharing/data; directory mismatch; zero-byte enumeration; delete/recreate isolation; common attributes. |
| Locks | 39 | All 14 distinct zero-length table tuples, each on same/other FileId; six unsigned 64-bit boundary partitions; EOF truncation retention; zero-byte READ; blocked LOCK cancelled by CLOSE/TDIS/LOGOFF. Repeated source rows are not repeated maintained cases. |
| Durable grant matrices | 138 | All 4 oplock by 8 sharing and 5 lease-state by 8 sharing rows for v1/v2, excluding 6 exact share=7 grants already exercised by P7c. CREATE response context presence/length, lease state, timeout and nonpersistent flag. This is ordinary in-memory SMB3 durability, not CA/persistent recovery. |
| Lease upgrades | 21 | Full 5-by-5 source state matrix; four numerical edges (5→1/3/7, 7→5) already in `upstream.state.lease.subset-sidegrade`. READ=1, HANDLE=2, WRITE=4. Historical prose calling state 5 RH is mislabeled; the exact numerical old assertions remain useful. |
| Copy | 9 | Multiple reordered chunks, byte chunks, overwrite/preserved tail, sparse target, backward same-open overlap, streams, distinct stable resume keys, bad key, partial source-EOF progress. Existing `p5.copy.*` maps separately cover forward overlap, append, lock denial, size limits and partial backend failures. |
| Ordinary replay flag | 9 | CREATE/READ/WRITE/FLUSH/QUERY/SET/LOCK/UNLOCK/CLOSE on a regular nondurable SMB300 open. No duplicate CreateGuid/grant claim. SET position is checked by subsequent QUERY. |
| TCP connection/tree/session/auth | 16 | Full two-open connect/lifetime chain; invalid/other TreeId and invalid SessionId with no mutation; independent logoffs; PreviousSessionId retirement; reauth/key retention; invalid credentials/token/order with valid retry; SPNEGO roundtrip; unsigned/tampered/bad-MAC WRITE; negotiated NTLM SIGN flag. |

These are protocol matrix rows, not 751 wholly unrelated workflows. Different
access bits, sharing directions, stream dispositions, lease states, unsigned
range positions and CREATE option bits are independently observable inputs.
Within a case, setup/retry/cleanup assertions are not counted as separate tests.
No source table byte or helper function contributes a maintained test count.

CREATE expectations are checked against [MS-SMB2 2.2.13](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/e8fb45c1-a03d-44ca-b7ae-47385cfd7997).
In particular, DIRECTORY_FILE limits dispositions/options and prescribes file
collision precedence; OPEN_BY_FILE_ID and RESERVE_OPFILTER require NOT_SUPPORTED.
The Samba table's legacy 0x80 rejection is not treated as current normative SMB2.
Sharing expectations come independently from Samba's literal table, including
EXECUTE requiring read sharing and stat-only opens not reserving data access.
Attribute results come from `attr_results`/`test_create_gentest`, with the local
host limitation recorded above rather than silently adopting implementation
output.

Zero-length lock expectations are independently confirmed by the inclusive-last-
byte algorithm in [MS-FSA 2.1.4.10](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-fsa/124bb289-eeef-4653-b9c6-4fb93dd07a21).
A nonzero-offset zero-length range can intersect the strict interior of another
range; {0,0} does not. [MS-SMB2 processing locks](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/670c7eda-e683-4923-9477-414303959613)
also distinguishes mandatory behavior from SHOULD rejection policies. The old
compound error-propagation mismatch is retained explicitly in the inventory.

Lease and durable table translation uses the independent [lease wire bit definitions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/250a5100-f8b0-4b32-a202-f592ce4c05e7).
The auth negative-token test requires terminal failure, excluding
MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED; [MS-SMB2 3.3.5.5.3](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/5ed93f06-a1d2-4837-8954-fa8b833c2654)
propagates the authentication mechanism's error and does not mandate
LOGON_FAILURE for every malformed encoding.

## Evidence and fixture corrections

Set `SMB_VALIDATION_OUTPUT` to the new external campaign root. The reproducible
selection is `dotnet build tests/Smb.Tests -c Release --artifacts-path
"$SMB_VALIDATION_OUTPUT/samba/build-final-samba"`, then
`dotnet "$SMB_VALIDATION_OUTPUT/samba/build-final-samba/bin/Smb.Tests/release/Smb.Tests.dll"
--filter upstream.samba.`. Capture commands are also recorded as JSON argument
arrays; output files are never overwritten by a later capture.

- `samba/runs/first-core`: build blocked by another owner's in-progress Microsoft source errors; not behavioral RED.
- `samba/runs/first-complete`: own compile errors (buffer-offset type and span across await); not behavioral RED.
- `samba/runs/first-behavior`: first executable 467-case selection, 328 pass / 139 fail. Original log retained. Its two new test files were reconstructed after corrections and **verified byte-for-byte against their original pre-run SHA-256** in `reconstructed-test-sources/`; this is explicitly post-hoc reconstruction, not a claimed contemporaneous snapshot.
- `samba/runs/corrected-fixtures`: build blocked by a concurrently edited Microsoft AesGcm overload; not behavioral RED.
- `samba/runs/expanded-final`: 581 cases, 421 pass / 160 fail; exact source tar, hashes, patch and build identities retained.
- `samba/runs/durable-expanded`: 719 cases, 526 pass / 193 fail. The 32 inverted RH/RW grant expectations were a fixture bit-mapping error, not production RED; original log retained.
- `samba/runs/final-samba`: final 751 cases, 586 pass / 165 fail; build succeeded with zero warnings/errors. Complete relevant source tar, before/after SHA-256, working diff/status, argument arrays, loaded assembly identities/MVIDs/hashes and output retained. Every captured source hash is unchanged across this run.

Fixture corrections were explicit: querying EOF avoids an irrelevant locked-byte
READ before checking lock retention; stream-name/sharing/enumeration/attributes
cases now create their base through SMB first, isolating them from the separate
implicit-base-create RED; COPY between streams does the same. Invalid GSS token
statuses were originally overconstrained to LOGON_FAILURE; a malformed mechanism
must fail terminally with its own error. MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED has error
severity bits, so severity alone was corrected to exclude that continuation
status explicitly. Unsigned rejected requests need not receive the signed-success
response checks used by signed requests. The original TreeId test imposed a
single validation ordering that the source did not require; this fixture now
checks FILE_CLOSED after handle/tree association rejection and unchanged bytes.
Teardown's initial FILE_CLOSED assumption for an actively cancelled LOCK was
replaced with CANCELLED, matching cancellation semantics; the source's subsequent
post-teardown invalid-handle assertion is a different operation. Finally, the
initial new durable fixture accidentally used WRITE=2/HANDLE=4; independent wire
definitions establish HANDLE=2/WRITE=4. No production expectation was changed
merely to make a failing test pass.

## Fresh external authentication reproduction

`external-auth/` contains a freshly built maintained .NET host, disposable share,
new client logs and command manifests. Existing portable Samba tools were copied
into that new directory; no old evidence or repository tools were changed.
The external binaries identify themselves as **Samba 4.19.5-Ubuntu**, not the pinned
source revision, so this is explicitly a separately versioned interoperability
probe. `smbclient` with `--client-protection=sign`, SMB2_02 fixed on both ends,
fails during SPNEGO/NTLM with missing `NTLMSSP_NEGOTIATE_SIGN`, code `0x80090302`.
A corrected `smbtorture -p <ephemeral-port> ... smb2.connect` independently fails
share connection with that code before file-operation assertions. The initial
unsupported `--port` spelling is retained separately as a tool-invocation error.
Required signing and the default server security configuration were preserved.
`upstream.samba.auth.ntlm-negotiated-sign-flag` reproduces the dropped challenge
bit through an independent raw real-TCP exchange. Full original upstream
execution remains blocked by this auth failure; the local adapted cases do run.

## Serial repair candidates (production deliberately untouched)

| Representative exact RED | Observed behavior / diagnosis | Recommended narrow repair |
|---|---|---|
| `upstream.samba.share.share-access.2.20` and `.access-share.2.20` | EXECUTE accepted when read sharing absent. `MapAccess` omits FILE_EXECUTE. Ten matrix failures exercise EXECUTE alone or combined with WRITE, in both directions. | Include executable access in read-sharing semantics without conflating attribute-only opens with data reads. |
| `upstream.samba.share.stat-open-share-none` | Read-attributes-only open with share-none incorrectly blocks later data open. | Separate sharing access intent from the OS handle's minimal read access, and apply stat-only exemption. |
| `upstream.samba.attr.create.2` / `.attr.overwrite.2.80` | Requested CREATE DOS attributes ignored; basic hidden/system SET does not survive host representation, then overwrite bypasses required matching. 120 rows fail. | Review host-backed vs per-object metadata policy, apply CREATE attributes, preserve requested supported DOS flags, and validate overwrite before truncation. Do not claim full NTFS persistence. Treat automatic ARCHIVE/unsupported DOS bits with explicit specification/host review. |
| `upstream.samba.directory.option.4` (also 8/800/200) | Invalid directory option combination accepted. | Validate allowed DIRECTORY_FILE combinations before store mutation. |
| `upstream.samba.directory.mkdir-collision-file` | NOT_A_DIRECTORY returned instead of CREATE collision precedence. | Check CREATE/name collision before non-directory mismatch. |
| `upstream.samba.directory.missing-parent` | Directory.CreateDirectory recursively manufactures absent parent. | Require existing parent before new directory creation. |
| `upstream.samba.directory.temporary-attribute` | SET temporary flag on a directory falsely succeeds. | Reject invalid directory metadata before applying fields. |
| `upstream.samba.create.option-bit.00002000` and `.00100000` | OPEN_BY_FILE_ID / RESERVE_OPFILTER accepted. | Return required NOT_SUPPORTED before opening/creating backend object. |
| `upstream.samba.create.reserved-access.04000000` / `.08000000` | Wire status is ACCESS_DENIED, but denied CREATE leaves a new file on disk. | Validate reserved access before backend CREATE, or rollback the newly created object safely; never delete a preexisting object. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.base-overwrite.{0,4,5}` | Base truncation leaves named streams. | Apply stream-removal semantics to successful base overwrite/supersede while preserving old-open identity rules. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.invalid-type.*` | Invalid stream type produces NAME_NOT_FOUND instead of NAME_INVALID. | Validate stream suffix/type before splitting or looking up base. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.implicit-base-create` | CREATE named stream on missing base fails. | Atomically create eligible base for stream CREATE/OPEN_IF, with cleanup if stream creation fails. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.directory-option` | Named stream with DIRECTORY_FILE returns missing-base status. | Reject named-directory-stream type before base lookup. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.base-delete-all-streams` | New base with recycled name inherits old object's named stream. | Clear stream metadata by retired object identity on final base deletion; prevent path-key reuse from exposing old state. |
| `upstream.samba.stream.attributes-shared` | Stream/basic attributes do not reflect base attribute state. | Query/set common file metadata through base object identity. |
| `upstream.samba.lock.zero.10.0.9.2.False` (8 variants) | Zero-length ranges are globally treated as nonoverlapping. | Implement zero-length strict-interior overlap, preserving special {0,0} and unsigned-end arithmetic. |
| `upstream.samba.replay.regular.set` | FilePositionInformation SET returns success but subsequent QUERY returns zero. | Store position on the open and serialize it through QUERY; replay flag is unrelated to this core state defect. |
| `upstream.samba.session.previous-session-reconnect` | New authenticated PreviousSessionId session leaves old session usable. | Retire the previous authorized session and its resources after successful replacement auth, preserving identity checks. |
| `upstream.samba.session.reauth-preserves-key` | Established-session reauth unconditionally denied at first token. | Implement same-session reauth transition and required original key/open lifetime, with failures isolated according to protocol. |
| `upstream.samba.auth.type3-first` | First NTLM TYPE3 gets a challenge instead of terminal rejection. | Validate the expected NTLM message type before entering challenge stage. |
| `upstream.samba.auth.ntlm-negotiated-sign-flag` | Requested NTLM SIGN bit dropped from CHALLENGE. Same missing flag blocks real Samba. | Negotiate NTLM flags from valid client NEGOTIATE plus server requirements; verify unchanged signing-required external roundtrip after repair. |

The next section records every final failing name and original assertion text.
The failures are not expected-failure passes and the tests are not skipped.

## Completeness boundary and open coverage

The ledger inventories **822 boolean C functions**, including **694 registered
function definitions**, every one of the **695 registration calls**, and
**75 initializer tables/vectors with 1,768 source entries**. Helpers and vector
bytes are explicitly separate from protocol cases. Source branches and repeated
load loops are not invented local passes. The detailed function dispositions are
41 adapted here (often named subcases), 29 with exact previously covered subcases,
71 unsupported-feature functions, 21 external/platform blockers, 13 client/harness
functions, 9 normative mismatches, and **510 remaining registered definitions**.
Therefore this is a broad tested addition and an exhaustive symbol/table ledger,
**not completion of all feasible translations or a whole-suite pass**. In
particular, ordinary remaining compound permutations, stream rename/type variants,
lease conflict/break sequences, durable reconnect/lock combinations, information-
class/access subcases, notify scenarios, namespace permutations and session
signing/encryption variants must not be dismissed because a related local test
exists. Those are openly recorded translation gaps; absence of native Windows,
KDC or external auth success does not make their feasible local wire adaptations
impossible. Parent/source-owner integration must resolve them before claiming the
requested all-feasible campaign complete.

## Complete C-function inventory

Rows identify definitions, including test helpers; registration names come from every `torture_suite_add_*` call, including smb2.c cross-file registrations. A mapped **subcase** is not a whole-function pass. `remaining` is an open coverage gap, never a pass or an infrastructure excuse.

### connect.c

| Symbol / registration | Disposition | Exact mapping or reason |
|---|---|---|
adapted here | `upstream.samba.session.connect-source-workflow`: two opens, two 64KiB writes/ALL_INFORMATION, flush/read bytes, full closes, stale close/TDIS/LOGOFF and sessionless keepalive. | `upstream.samba.connect.full-lifetime`: two opens, WRITE/FLUSH/READ/full CLOSE/stale CLOSE/double TDIS/double LOGOFF; keepalive transport branch remains |

## Material table/vector inventory

Each initializer entry below is a source input row/vector, **not** a registered local test. Repeated literals are retained as upstream positions; maintained tests deduplicate equivalent scenarios. Numerical buffers/Unicode tokens are protocol vectors where used by registered functions; harness-only arrays do not add coverage. Every table is linked at its definition, and each row is linked at its source position. Status assertions and operation sequences inside functions remain at the function permalinks above.

## Serial authentication and session repair (2026-11-08)

The earlier 751-case port-only results above are historical. This subsequent
allocation repairs production authentication/session code and adds **60 cases**
in `UpstreamSambaSessionTests.cs`, registered through the existing partial class.
It does not change file/create/locking/metadata production code or Go/Microsoft
expectations. The supported-session inventory above now has concrete mappings or
feature/infrastructure dispositions; unrelated inventory remains for its owners.
No Native Windows execution or final Native AOT gate is claimed here.

Evidence is in the campaign's new `auth-session-repair/` directory. Each capture
contains before/after source hashes, source archive, original checkout diff,
exact build/run argument arrays, complete logs and printed loaded assembly
identities. `final-verified/` has no production drift. The capture's external
`focused-tests.targets` excludes only the separate, still-unregistered
`UpstreamSambaMore*.cs` work in progress; it excludes none of the registered
Samba/Go/Microsoft selections or prior regression cases. Several preceding build
logs retain that other allocation's incomplete partial-class compilation, two
failed attempts at an external-only exclusion switch, and one local undefined
status-enum spelling. These are build failures, not protocol results. No checkout
restore, staging, commit, runtime download or ordinary-test dependency was added.

### Repaired behavior and normative basis

- NTLM NEGOTIATE is decoded before generating a challenge. Fixed fields, optional
  Version and supplied security-buffer bounds are validated; wrong-stage TYPE2/
  TYPE3 and unsupported OEM-only input terminally fail. Supported requested
  SIGN/SEAL/ESS/KEY_EXCH flags are negotiated without reflecting reserved, LM-key,
  OEM or unsupported Version bits. Strength flags depend on SIGN/SEAL. This follows
  [MS-NLMP 3.2.5.1.1](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-nlmp/81d15e3e-3ac3-41f0-920d-846149f3a814)
  and [2.2.2.5](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-nlmp/99d90ff4-957f-4c8a-80e4-5bfe5a9a9832).
- KEY_EXCH requires a negotiated capability and a 16-byte encrypted exported key.
  NTLMv2 client challenges require a bounded, terminated AV list, while reserved
  fields are ignored as required. The exact Samba `ntlmssp_bug14932` diagnostic
  payload now fails INVALID_PARAMETER before proof validation, following
  [MS-NLMP 2.2.2.7](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-nlmp/aee311d6-21a7-4470-92a5-c4ecb022a87b).
- A supplied SPNEGO mechanism-list MIC is verified even when the exchange is
  optional. The server returns the direction-specific MIC; empty/truncated/bad
  MICs fail. NTLM ESS signing honors negotiated KEY_EXCH and key strength.
  Independent test HMAC/MD5/RC4 calculations verify the returned token, per
  [RFC 4178 section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4178.html#section-5).
- Reauth uses a new authentication context serialized per session, verifies the
  original signing key, retains established identity, original session/channel
  keys, TreeIds and FileIds, and releases the completed context. Invalid reauth
  retires that session and its resources across channels; unrelated sessions
  survive. Successful same-user PreviousSessionId replacement retires the old
  session only after authentication, including sharing/delete-on-close cleanup.
  Foreign, missing and self PreviousSessionIds are ignored. See
  [MS-SMB2 3.3.5.5.2](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/5ecc02fb-0e60-4cba-afeb-f13100a6e65e)
  and [3.3.5.5.3](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/5ed93f06-a1d2-4837-8954-fa8b833c2654).
- Binding derives a new channel signing key from the binding authentication
  result, leaving the original session key intact. Binding to a session whose
  final channel is draining returns USER_SESSION_DELETED. Unsigned binding,
  in-progress/expired session state and identity mismatch use the respective
  normative statuses in
  [MS-SMB2 3.3.5.5](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/e545352b-9f2b-4c5e-9350-db46e4f6755e).
  The pinned Samba different-user ACCESS_DENIED expectation differs from this
  section's NOT_SUPPORTED requirement; the adapted case uses the latter, with
  the original source and failed evidence retained. No denied request is allowed
  to proceed. The shared `WireClient.BindAsync` fixture is narrowly corrected to
  derive its new channel key from its own NTLM result on SMB300/302 as well.
- With the user's subsequent encryption-boundary ownership extension, initial
  SESSION_SETUP remains plain under required encryption, invalid authenticated
  transforms close the transport, and every encrypted response/compound inner
  header has zero signature and no SIGNED bit. Required-encryption policy remains
  enabled for the successful protected file workflow. These repairs follow
  [MS-SMB2 3.3.4.1.4](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/c4a91ca0-8d2e-404c-b9ac-3783ba9a01ef)
  and [3.3.5.2.1.1](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/94398d18-48ff-4b6c-a32d-0154b2e88238).
  Only `SmbConnectionHandler`'s encryption boundary is changed.

### RED/GREEN and fixture corrections

`original-red/` reproduces all four handed-off failures: auth **7/9**, session
**1/3**. The original peer omitted PreviousSessionId from the final authentication
request and incorrectly required a reauth challenge to be signed. Those fixture
mistakes are corrected from the specification; `corrected-red-baseline/` rebuilds
an external archive of the original production source with the corrected new
fixtures: auth **7/27**, session **4/9**. Both original evidence and the corrected
RED are retained. The initial malformed-token severity check also accidentally
included MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED; the corrected check explicitly excludes it.
This was a test-oracle correction, not a new production pass.

`mic-red/` has **0/3** before SPNEGO repair; `mic-empty-red/` separately preserves
acceptance of an empty MIC without negotiated signing. `binding-key-red/` has
**0/2** for the independently checked new channel key. `shutdown-status-capture/`
has **0/1** for a bind receiving MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED while the final physical
READ is still draining. Each repaired case passes in `final-verified/`.

`transform-red/` preserves Go encrypted selection **1/4** (the required setup
failures and optional bad-tag timeout) and Microsoft encrypted selection **0/7**
(nonzero inner signatures). Unchanged Go auth tests now pass **29/29**; Microsoft
setup/encrypted/compound/binding regressions pass except the separate global
client-dialect finding below. No retry converts a failed result into a pass.

New source workflows use real LocalFileStore where sharing, directory handling
or delete-on-close matters. Initial FakeFileStore attempts and their unsupported
operation results remain in the capture logs. The cached reconnect initially
requested a grant on a backend without the exclusive-access contract: granting
NONE there was correct. `CachedReconnect` now uses `P7cTests.Root.Store()` with
explicit exclusive server access, and the batch grant/replacement workflow
passes. Similarly, direct binding fixtures now seed a coherent SMB3 signing key
instead of treating an all-zero base key as its derived signing key. These are
fixture corrections, not caching/file production edits.

### Final maintained selections and remaining findings

`final-verified/` records:

| Selection | Passed / total | Remaining result |
|---|---:|---|
| Samba auth | 31 / 31 | All selected repaired auth cases pass |
| Samba session/connect/tcon/smb3error | 40 / 41 | The pinned OS2 legacy filename expectation returns SUCCESS instead of OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND; retained RED, no mandatory-protocol defect inferred from this legacy policy alone |
| Go auth/framing/encryption | 29 / 29 | Includes optional/required encrypted roundtrip and corrupt-tag disconnect |
| Microsoft auth | 32 / 33 | Conflicting non-SMB202 dialects under the same client GUID are accepted |
| Prior wire and P1–P8 selections | 853 / 854 | `p5.metadata.volume-size` expects host capacity 480305459 allocation units, receives 480430792 with campaign TMPDIR on the external volume; unrelated host-volume fixture/metadata finding retained |

The Microsoft dialect RED requires a server-wide client-GUID/dialect record, with
atomic publication and lifecycle rules across connections. A special-case
PreviousSessionId comparison would not implement the required GlobalClientTable.
That stateful negotiation finding is retained for coherent serial work; this patch
does not claim full negotiation/authentication conformance.

The session source inventory no longer labels anonymous cases as generic
"remaining" or auth-excluded. Reauth2–5 and anonymous encryption/signing require a
successful anonymous NTLM context and IPC$ (plus owner/DACL and parent-rename
semantics for reauth3–5), unavailable under the retained backend/policy. They have
precise feature-gap dispositions, not passing or KDC claims. Kerberos expiry
requires actual KDC credentials/ticket-lifetime control and remains an explicit
infrastructure blocker. SMB311 multichannel bind matrices are restricted by the
actual `SupportsMultichannel` profile gate; their exact dialect, algorithm, cipher,
GUID and expected-status parameters are preserved in
`session-bind-source-parameters.json`. Standalone SMB311 signing and encryption
are executed through real TCP for all three signing algorithms and four ciphers.
The two source `wrong_session_key` initializers are each one 16-byte vector;
their first three bytes are not three independent tests.

### Fresh native clients

`external-verified/` contains a fresh maintained host build and new share, exact
commands, complete client/server logs and result JSON. Samba **4.19.5-Ubuntu
smbclient**, with `--client-protection=sign`, passes upload/download/rename/list/
delete; uploaded and downloaded bytes match. The original missing-SIGN failure
advanced to a newly observed missing-SPNEGO-MIC failure (`external/`), then passed
after MIC repair (`external-mic/`, `external-verified/`). All attempts remain.

Fresh native hirochachacha Go client authenticated CREATE/WRITE/READ passes on all
five dialects: SMB202, SMB210, SMB300, SMB302 and SMB311. No client/server security
setting is relaxed. These are external execution rows, not additional maintained
cases or a whole-suite pass.

Native `smbtorture smb2.connect` now authenticates and completes the two-open
64KiB write/query/flush/read/close/TDIS workflow. It still fails its final repeated
LOGOFF assertion: the server log reports USER_SESSION_DELETED, while the signing-
required client reports ACCESS_DENIED for that unsigned error. The local exact
status workflow passes. This remaining error-response interoperability discrepancy
is concrete and retained; neither a fabricated SIGNED flag without a key nor a
weakened native client configuration is used to label it green.

`integrated-final/` subsequently builds the shared checkout with **zero warnings
and zero errors, without the temporary compile exclusion**. It repeats Samba
auth **31/31**, Samba session **40/41**, Go auth **29/29**, Microsoft auth **32/33**
and P7b **44/44**, with the same two upstream findings and no production drift.
`required-go/` separately rebuilds the current host with RequireEncryption=true
and runs the pinned native CloudSoda integration binary: **16/16 selected test
functions pass** on SMB302, including their file/directory/subcase workflows.
The exact configuration, binary command, full test log and host log are retained.
This replaces the old native encrypted pre-auth blocker for that selection;
it is not a whole upstream suite or Windows/AOT result.

`affected-connect-tcon/` additionally reruns the four pre-existing signed TCP
connect/tree-ID/session-ID cases against the integrated-final binary: **4/4 pass**.
